# Link-level names the compiler emits that the IDA database spells differently
# (usually sub_/dword_) or not at all. These are the libraries' own public
# names, not new names for game code. Each needs evidence.
#
# NAME  ADDRESS  EVIDENCE

___security_cookie          0x00435810  libcmt.lib seccook.obj .data (4EE640BB) is identical to the image at 0x435810

_asin                       0x002C6AC0  libcmt.lib asin.obj .text (__CIasin at +0, _asin at +0x14, 203 bytes) is located at 0x2C6AAC by tools/lib_scan.py with its relocations masked
_acos                       0x002C6B8C  libcmt.lib acos.obj .text (__CIacos at +0, _acos at +0x14, 203 bytes) is located at 0x2C6B78 by tools/lib_scan.py with its relocations masked; sub_D45F0 calls _acos with a double on the stack
_atan                       0x002C773C  libcmt.lib atan.obj .text (_atan at +0, __CIatan at +4, __atan_default at +0x1A, 165 bytes) is located at 0x2C773C by tools/lib_scan.py with its relocations masked; the database names it atanf; sub_D45F0 calls it with a double on the stack
_fmod                       0x002C7CE4  libcmt.lib 87fmod.obj .text (_fmod at +0, __CIfmod at +0xA, 34 bytes) is located at 0x2C7CE4 by tools/lib_scan.py with its relocations masked; the database names it sub_2C7CE4; sub_13F9E0 calls it with two doubles on the stack
_memcpy                     0x002C5D80  libcmt.lib memcpy.obj and memmove.obj are byte-identical (829 bytes, one source) and both match at 0x2C5D80 and 0x2C66B0; every located library call to _memmove reaches 0x2C66B0, so 0x2C5D80 (the database's memmove_x) is _memcpy
